Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating crackers or sweets, does it ever feel like you can’t stop or control how much you’re eating?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like saying it that way, like I’m out of control, but sometimes I’ll be standing at the counter with crackers or something sweet and I keep taking more even though it doesn’t really taste good. Other days I’ll forget lunch altogether, so it’s just gotten sort of mixed up. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I suppose some days it is less, yes… I’ll realize I’ve gone right past lunch and didn’t really think about eating. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I suppose some days it is less, yes… I’ll realize I’ve gone right past lunch and didn’t really think about eating. It’s more that I’ll stand there and keep taking crackers or something sweet, more than I meant to, even though nothing tastes right. And then on other days I’ll forget lunch completely. It’s just gotten all turned around, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this low nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the color has gone out of the day before I even get dressed. It’s been happening pretty regularly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it unusual, exactly… I mean, I am older, so I expect to slow down some. But yes, I suppose I’ve felt a bit more worn out than I’d like lately. Not completely unable to do things, just… low energy, if that’s an acceptable way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling especially anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think especially anxious, no… not in a way that stands out. I worry some, of course, but I suppose everyone does. It hasn’t been the main thing I’ve noticed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or like you’re a burden to other people lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say worthless… that sounds a bit strong. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.9, R63.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose it’s my eating, mostly. It’s gotten a little turned around lately. Some days I realize I’ve forgotten lunch altogether, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. Not a proper meal, I know. And nothing really tastes quite right. I’m not sure if that’s the sort of thing I should be worried about, but it’s what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make too much of it, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling. Like the color’s gone out of the day before I’ve even gotten dressed. I suppose that counts as feeling down, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit, I suppose. I used to be quite good about going to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club. I’d have my little bag ready by the door.\n\nLately, though, it just sits there. I tell myself I’ll go, but when it’s time, I can’t seem to make myself care enough to leave. That sounds bad, doesn’t it? I don’t mean to be lazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I’m going to do anything to myself. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.\n\nBut sometimes, when I’m very tired, I do catch myself thinking… if I just didn’t wake up one morning, maybe that would be a relief. For everyone, I mean. I know that sounds awful. I’m sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping more or less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, really… I haven’t kept track the way I probably should. Some nights feel a bit unsettled, but I couldn’t honestly say if it’s more or less than usual. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, at my age I’m not exactly worrying about my figure the way I might’ve years ago. I haven’t really been afraid of gaining weight, no. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating crackers or sweets, does it ever feel like you can’t stop or control how much you’re eating?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like saying it that way, like I’m out of control, but sometimes I’ll be standing at the counter with crackers or something sweet and I keep taking more even though it doesn’t really taste good. Other days I’ll forget lunch altogether, so it’s just gotten sort of mixed up. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I suppose some days it is less, yes… I’ll realize I’ve gone right past lunch and didn’t really think about eating. But then other days I pick at crackers or sweets at the counter, so it’s not a neat answer. It’s more like my eating has gotten turned around, if that makes sense. Nothing tastes quite right lately.\n\nDoctor: When that happens at the counter, do you eat what most people would consider a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Not usually a huge amount, I don’t think… not like a whole meal’s worth, exactly. It’s more that I’ll stand there and keep taking crackers or something sweet, more than I meant to, even though nothing tastes right. And then on other days I’ll forget lunch completely. It’s just gotten all turned around, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this low nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the color has gone out of the day before I even get dressed. It’s been happening pretty regularly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it unusual, exactly… I mean, I am older, so I expect to slow down some. But yes, I suppose I’ve felt a bit more worn out than I’d like lately. Not completely unable to do things, just… low energy, if that’s an acceptable way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling especially anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think especially anxious, no… not in a way that stands out. I worry some, of course, but I suppose everyone does. It hasn’t been the main thing I’ve noticed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or like you’re a burden to other people lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say worthless… that sounds a bit strong. I suppose I sometimes feel like I’m not very useful anymore, but I try not to put that on other people. I don’t think I’m a burden, at least I hope I’m not. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I do notice it. Sometimes I’ll sit there with simple things in front of me, like whether I should call the pharmacy first or open the mail, and my mind just gets foggy. Then I end up not doing either one for a while. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: A bit, I suppose… I mean, I’m not just sitting there frozen or anything. But my body does feel slowed down lately. It can take me longer than it should to get across the room or button my sweater, little things like that. And then other times I’ll find myself pacing around with the same papers in my hand and not really getting anywhere. I don’t know if that counts as “noticeably,” but I have noticed it some.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose it’s my eating, mostly. It’s gotten a little turned around lately. Some days I realize I’ve forgotten lunch altogether, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. Not a proper meal, I know. And nothing really tastes quite right. I’m not sure if that’s the sort of thing I should be worried about, but it’s what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make too much of it, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling. Like the color’s gone out of the day before I’ve even gotten dressed. I suppose that counts as feeling down, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit, I suppose. I used to be quite good about going to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club. I’d have my little bag ready by the door.\n\nLately, though, it just sits there. I tell myself I’ll go, but when it’s time, I can’t seem to make myself care enough to leave. That sounds bad, doesn’t it? I don’t mean to be lazy.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say I’m going to do anything to myself. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.\n\nBut sometimes, when I’m very tired, I do catch myself thinking… if I just didn’t wake up one morning, maybe that would be a relief. For everyone, I mean. I know that sounds awful. I’m sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping more or less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, really… I haven’t kept track the way I probably should. Some nights feel a bit unsettled, but I couldn’t honestly say if it’s more or less than usual. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, at my age I’m not exactly worrying about my figure the way I might’ve years ago. I haven’t really been afraid of gaining weight, no. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating crackers or sweets, does it ever feel like you can’t stop or control how much you’re eating?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like saying it that way, like I’m out of control, but sometimes I’ll be standing at the counter with crackers or something sweet and I keep taking more even though it doesn’t really taste good. Other days I’ll forget lunch altogether, so it’s just gotten sort of mixed up. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I suppose some days it is less, yes… I’ll realize I’ve gone right past lunch and didn’t really think about eating. But then other days I pick at crackers or sweets at the counter, so it’s not a neat answer. It’s more like my eating has gotten turned around, if that makes sense. Nothing tastes quite right lately.\n\nDoctor: When that happens at the counter, do you eat what most people would consider a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Not usually a huge amount, I don’t think… not like a whole meal’s worth, exactly. It’s just gotten all turned around, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this low nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the color has gone out of the day before I even get dressed. It’s been happening pretty regularly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it unusual, exactly… I mean, I am older, so I expect to slow down some. But yes, I suppose I’ve felt a bit more worn out than I’d like lately. I suppose I sometimes feel like I’m not very useful anymore, but I try not to put that on other people. I don’t think I’m a burden, at least I hope I’m not. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I do notice it.
